ACPI spec Appendix B)\n * are available, video.ko should be used to handle the device.\n *\n * Otherwise vendor specific drivers like thinkpad_acpi, asus-laptop,\n * sony_acpi,... can take care about Specifically, how does the kernel decide whether to use native, vendor, or video backlight drivers for laptops like Acer? I need to understand the acpi_backlight parameter handling and the video_detect.c logic.\\nshare_url: https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/deepsearch/cc510e04-1d4d-40ba-9476-8ec1c62f04cb\\ncreated: 2026-02-06T17:51:29Z\\nupdated: 2026-02-06T17:52:31Z\\n---\\n\\n# How does ACPI video backlight detection work in the Linux kernel? Specifically, how does the kernel decide whether to use native, vendor, or video backlight drivers for laptops like Acer? I need to understand the acpi_backlight parameter handling and the video_detect.c logic.\\n\\nPerfect! Now I have all the key information. Let me provide a comprehensive explanation of how ACPI video backlight detection works in the Linux kernel.\\n\\n## ACPI Video Backlight Detection in Linux\\n\\nThe Linux kernel uses a sophisticated decision tree in [drivers/acpi/video_detect.c](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c) to determine which backlight driver to use. Here's how it works:\\n\\n### Backlight Type Options\\n\\nThe kernel can choose from several backlight types, defined in [include/acpi/video.h](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/include/acpi/video.h?L45-L54):\\n\\n```c\\nenum acpi_backlight_type {\\n    acpi_backlight_undef = -1,\\n    acpi_backlight_none = 0,\\n    acpi_backlight_video,      // ACPI video interface\\n    acpi_backlight_vendor,     // Vendor-specific (e.g., thinkpad_acpi)\\n    acpi_backlight_native,     // GPU driver's native backlight\\n    acpi_backlight_nvidia_wmi_ec,\\n    acpi_backlight_apple_gmux,\\n    acpi_backlight_dell_uart,\\n};\\n```\\n\\n### acpi_backlight Parameter Handling\\n\\nThe `acpi_backlight=` kernel parameter is handled in two places:\\n\\n1. **Parameter setup** in [drivers/acpi/utils.c](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/utils.c?L1035-L1048):\\n```c\\nchar acpi_video_backlight_string[16];\\nstatic int __init acpi_backlight(char *str) {\\n    strscpy(acpi_video_backlight_string, str, sizeof(acpi_video_backlight_string));\\n    return 1;\\n}\\n__setup(\\\"acpi_backlight=\\\", acpi_backlight);\\n```\\n\\n2. **Parameter parsing** in [video_detect.c](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L45-L61):\\n```c\\nstatic void acpi_video_parse_cmdline(void) {\\n    if (!strcmp(\\\"vendor\\\", acpi_video_backlight_string))\\n        acpi_backlight_cmdline = acpi_backlight_vendor;\\n    if (!strcmp(\\\"video\\\", acpi_video_backlight_string))\\n        acpi_backlight_cmdline = acpi_backlight_video;\\n    if (!strcmp(\\\"native\\\", acpi_video_backlight_string))\\n        acpi_backlight_cmdline = acpi_backlight_native;\\n    // ... other options\\n}\\n```\\n\\n### Decision Logic in __acpi_video_get_backlight_type()\\n\\nThe core decision logic is in [video_detect.c](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L981-L1067) at `__acpi_video_get_backlight_type()`. The precedence order is:\\n\\n**1. Command line parameter** (highest priority)\\n```c\\nif (acpi_backlight_cmdline != acpi_backlight_undef)\\n    return acpi_backlight_cmdline;\\n```\\n\\n**2. DMI quirks** - Hardware-specific overrides for known problematic laptops\\n\\n**3. Special cases:**\\n- NVIDIA WMI EC backlight\\n- Apple gmux\\n- Dell UART backlight\\n\\n**4. ACPI video (unless native is preferred):**\\n```c\\nif ((video_caps & ACPI_VIDEO_BACKLIGHT) &&\\n    !(native_available && prefer_native_over_acpi_video()))\\n    return acpi_backlight_video;\\n```\\n\\n**5. Native GPU backlight** if available\\n\\n**6. Fallback:**\\n- On Windows 8+ systems: `acpi_backlight_none`\\n- On older systems: `acpi_backlight_vendor`\\n\\n### Windows 8+ Heuristic\\n\\nThe kernel prefers native backlight on Windows 8+ systems and Chromebooks ([video_detect.c L972-975](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L972-L975)):\\n\\n```c\\nstatic bool prefer_native_over_acpi_video(void) {\\n    return acpi_osi_is_win8() || google_cros_ec_present();\\n}\\n```\\n\\nThis is because Windows 8+ no longer uses ACPI video, so manufacturers stopped testing it properly.\\n\\n### Acer-Specific Quirks\\n\\nThe DMI quirk table includes several Acer models:\\n\\n**Acer KAV80** ([L150-158](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L150-L158)) - Forces **vendor** mode due to broken ACPI video backlight\\n\\n**Acer models forced to native** mode due to working native backlight:\\n- [Aspire 3830TG](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L644-L649)\\n- [Aspire 4810T](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L652-L657)\\n- [Aspire 5738z](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L660-L666)\\n- [Aspire 5741](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L670-L675)\\n- [Aspire 5750](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L679-L684)\\n- [Extensa 5235](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L688-L693)\\n- [TravelMate 4750](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L696-L701)\\n- [TravelMate 5735Z](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L704-L711)\\n- [TravelMate 5760](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L714-L720)\\n\\n### Summary\\n\\nThe backlight detection works by:\\n1.   acpi_backlight_none = 0,\\n    acpi_backlight_video,      // ACPI video interface\\n    acpi_backlight_vendor,     // Vendor-specific (e.g., thinkpad_acpi)\\n    acpi_backlight_native,     // GPU driver's native backlight\\n    acpi_backlight_nvidia_wmi_ec,\\n    acpi_backlight_apple_gmux,\\n    acpi_backlight_dell_uart,\\n};\\n```\\n\\n### acpi_backlight Parameter Handling\\n\\nThe Specifically, how does the kernel decide whether to use native, vendor, or video backlight drivers for laptops like Acer? I need to understand the acpi_backlight parameter handling and the video_detect.c logic.\n\nPerfect! Now I have all the key information. Let me provide a comprehensive explanation of how ACPI video backlight detection works in the Linux kernel.\n\n## ACPI Video Backlight Detection in Linux\n\nThe Linux kernel uses a sophisticated decision tree in [drivers/acpi/video_detect.c](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c) to determine which backlight driver to use. Here's how it works:\n\n### Backlight Type Options\n\nThe kernel can choose from several backlight types, defined in [include/acpi/video.h](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/include/acpi/video.h?L45-L54):\n\n```c\nenum acpi_backlight_type {\n    acpi_backlight_undef = -1,\n    acpi_backlight_none = 0,\n    acpi_backlight_video,      // ACPI video interface\n    acpi_backlight_vendor,     // Vendor-specific (e.g., thinkpad_acpi)\n    acpi_backlight_native,     // GPU driver's native backlight\n    acpi_backlight_nvidia_wmi_ec,\n    acpi_backlight_apple_gmux,\n    acpi_backlight_dell_uart,\n};\n```\n\n### acpi_backlight Parameter Handling\n\nThe `acpi_backlight=` kernel parameter is handled in two places:\n\n1. **Parameter setup** in [drivers/acpi/utils.c](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/utils.c?L1035-L1048):\n```c\nchar acpi_video_backlight_string[16];\nstatic int __init acpi_backlight(char *str) {\n    strscpy(acpi_video_backlight_string, str, sizeof(acpi_video_backlight_string));\n    return 1;\n}\n__setup(\"acpi_backlight=\", acpi_backlight);\n```\n\n2. **Parameter parsing** in [video_detect.c](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L45-L61):\n```c\nstatic void acpi_video_parse_cmdline(void) {\n    if (!strcmp(\"vendor\", acpi_video_backlight_string))\n        acpi_backlight_cmdline = acpi_backlight_vendor;\n    if (!strcmp(\"video\", acpi_video_backlight_string))\n        acpi_backlight_cmdline = acpi_backlight_video;\n    if (!strcmp(\"native\", acpi_video_backlight_string))\n        acpi_backlight_cmdline = acpi_backlight_native;\n    // ... other options\n}\n```\n\n### Decision Logic in __acpi_video_get_backlight_type()\n\nThe core decision logic is in [video_detect.c](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L981-L1067) at `__acpi_video_get_backlight_type()`. The precedence order is:\n\n**1. Command line parameter** (highest priority)\n```c\nif (acpi_backlight_cmdline != acpi_backlight_undef)\n    return acpi_backlight_cmdline;\n```\n\n**2. DMI quirks** - Hardware-specific overrides for known problematic laptops\n\n**3. Special cases:**\n- NVIDIA WMI EC backlight\n- Apple gmux\n- Dell UART backlight\n\n**4. ACPI video (unless native is preferred):**\n```c\nif ((video_caps & ACPI_VIDEO_BACKLIGHT) &&\n    !(native_available && prefer_native_over_acpi_video()))\n    return acpi_backlight_video;\n```\n\n**5. Native GPU backlight** if available\n\n**6. Fallback:**\n- On Windows 8+ systems: `acpi_backlight_none`\n- On older systems: `acpi_backlight_vendor`\n\n### Windows 8+ Heuristic\n\nThe kernel prefers native backlight on Windows 8+ systems and Chromebooks ([video_detect.c L972-975](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L972-L975)):\n\n```c\nstatic bool prefer_native_over_acpi_video(void) {\n    return acpi_osi_is_win8() || google_cros_ec_present();\n}\n```\n\nThis is because Windows 8+ no longer uses ACPI video, so manufacturers stopped testing it properly.\n\n### Acer-Specific Quirks\n\nThe DMI quirk table includes several Acer models:\n\n**Acer KAV80** ([L150-158](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L150-L158)) - Forces **vendor** mode due to broken ACPI video backlight\n\n**Acer models forced to native** mode due to working native backlight:\n- [Aspire 3830TG](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L644-L649)\n- [Aspire 4810T](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L652-L657)\n- [Aspire 5738z](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L660-L666)\n- [Aspire 5741](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L670-L675)\n- [Aspire 5750](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L679-L684)\n- [Extensa 5235](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L688-L693)\n- [TravelMate 4750](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L696-L701)\n- [TravelMate 5735Z](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L704-L711)\n- [TravelMate 5760](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c?L714-L720)\n\n### Summary\n\nThe backlight detection works by:\n1. Checking the `acpi_backlight=` kernel parameter first\n2. Applying DMI-based quirks for known problematic hardware\n3. Detecting special backlight controllers (nvidia-wmi-ec, gmux, etc.)\n4. Preferring native GPU backlight on modern (Win8+) systems\n5. Falling back to ACPI video or vendor-specific drivers on older hardware\n\nFor Acer laptops specifically, most modern models will use native backlight control, while the KAV80 specifically needs vendor mode. The system is designed to handle broken ACPI implementations on a per-model basis through DMI quirks.\n\n## Sources\n\n- [github.com/torvalds/linux/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c@b7ff715](/github.com/torvalds/linux@b7ff7151e653aa296ab6c5495b2c1ab7c21eb250/-/blob/drivers/acpi/video_detect.c)\n- [github.com/torvalds/linux/include/acpi/video.h@b7ff715](/github.com/torvalds/linux@b7ff7151e653aa296ab6c5495b2c1ab7c21eb250/-/blob/include/acpi/video.h)\n- [repo:torvalds/linux acpi_video_backlight_string __setup](/search?q=patterntype:keyword%20repo:torvalds%2Flinux%20acpi_video_backlight_string%20__setup)\n- [github.com/torvalds/linux/drivers/acpi/utils.c@b7ff715?L1030-1050](/github.com/torvalds/linux@b7ff7151e653aa296ab6c5495b2c1ab7c21eb250/-/blob/drivers/acpi/utils.c?L1030-1050)\n\n"}}}

Without this quirk, the kernel uses the default ACPI video backlight detection logic, which selects the wrong backlight interface for this hardware.\n\n**Evidence**:\n1. The bug report confirms that using `acpi_backlight=native` makes the brightness control work\n2. The local Linux 5.6.7 source has no entry for \"TravelMate 5735Z\" in the DMI quirk table\n3.
